8:T1472,HTTP contract of the headless `apps/tasks-api` (as amended by — dev port 3204; owner NexTasks, the seed of the Tasks application). It owns exactly
one record in: the `ApprovalRequest` (`nexus://tasks/approval/<id>`, resource type `tasks.approval`) —
the human decision a run waits for. Payload shapes reference JSON Schema components in ../schemas/v1/ — not
duplicated here.

Every operation runs the PEP pipeline (authenticate → tenancy → OpenFGA → OPA → execute → audit/outbox) through
core-api like every app; tasks-api holds no broker client for egress and no credentials. `ActorContext` is
derived server-side from the bearer; clients never send authority. Denials return coarse `reason_code` +
`user_message_key` (PepCheckResponse) — no policy text. Reason codes (`approval_decide`):
403 `AUTHOR_NOT_HUMAN` (`approval.not_human` — agents and services never decide, /02),
403 `NOT_DECIDER` (`approval.waiting_for_decider` — not in `deciders.user_ids`),
403 `SELF_APPROVAL` (`approval.self_approval` — the requester's own human unless `deciders.allow_self_approval`), 409 `APPROVAL_STATUS` (`approval.already_decided` — not pending / not approved for the
operation), 409 `APPROVAL_EXPIRED` (`approval.expired`), 409 `APPROVAL_CONSUMED` (a second
`consume`), 409 `APPROVAL_MISMATCH` (`approval.mismatch` — raised by core-api's executor when the
approval does not cover the call,; listed here so one vocabulary serves tests, UI and audit).

Who calls what: **services** create (`chat-api`, `chat-worker` — the caller that received
`approval_pending` or runs an `approval` node) and consume (`core-api`, before the adapter call); **humans**
decide and cancel — from the browser directly against this API with the same session bearer, never proxied
through chat-api; **reads**: a decider or the `requested_by.on_behalf_of` human, and same-tenant services —
tasks-api cannot evaluate `can_view` on a Project it does not own, so every other Project member sees the
approval through chat-api's projection on the tool call / run step (the U3 card without buttons). A decision emits
`nexus.tasks.approval.completed.v1` after the row commits (outbox); the chat-worker's `flow-approval-resumer`
turns it into exactly one `continue` (msg id `run:node:<approval uri>`). Expiry is a 30 s sweep (RLS GUC
row-restricted like ): `pending ∧ expires_at < now → expired` + `completed.v1 decision=expired` — it never
approves. Records carry arguments **redacted per the reader's clearance** and a digest of the full arguments;
secrets, tokens and raw payloads never appear.

**1.51.0 — VibeBan (A2).** The same process (`apps/tasks-api`, :3204,, database `nexus_tasks`) now owns the **work domain** behind the `TASKS_WORK_DOMAIN` flag:
WorkProject · WorkflowStage · ItemType · WorkItem · WorkRelation · Comment · Iteration (+ history, snapshots) · Milestone ·
ReferenceObject · ReferenceLink · ItemReference · ImportJob · AutomationBinding · ActivityEvent · SavedView · ProjectUpdate ·
Share, and the read models Progress · Inbox · My Work · Search · WorkContext · work-dataset publish. Every operation names
exactly one `TasksPepAction` in `x-nexus-pep` (approvals included — the matrix is
); `x-nexus-human-only: true` marks what an
agent or service principal is refused before FGA with 403 `AUTHOR_NOT_HUMAN`: create/share/administer/archive a
project, delete or share an item, start/complete/cancel an iteration, import docs, bind an automation, decide an approval.
Authorization = `WorkProject` relations `owner · editor · viewer · agent_member` in OpenFGA ∩ item shares (personal
items) ∩ classification ∩ state (archived → read-only); an organization administrator without a relation gets
counts only (`content_visible=false`). Hidden resources answer 404, forbidden actions 403.
Records carry `allowed_actions[]` decided server-side per reader; the Board never authorises client-side.
Reference bodies never cross this API and prompts/contexts carry URIs and short labels only. Every
ranking, suggestion, WIP or carry-over choice persists a `tasks-decision-record` whose `decision_id` the read
models return and the accepting/overriding operations take back — outcomes are written by the actions that exist, never by
a feedback control (owner deferral). Work-domain error codes (ErrorResponse, closed): 409 `RELATION_CYCLE` (`work.relation_cycle`),
409 `ITERATION_ACTIVE` (`work.iteration_active`), 409 `ITERATION_STATUS`, 409 `MILESTONE_STATUS`, 409
`VERSION_CONFLICT` (`work.stale` — `If-Match` ≠ `version`), 409 `PROJECT_ARCHIVED` (`work.archived`), 409
`PROJECT_KEY_TAKEN`, 409 `IMPORT_RUNNING`, 409 `STAGE_HAS_ITEMS`, 422 `DISPOSITION_REQUIRED`, 422
`STAGE_CATEGORY_REQUIRED`, 422 `REFERENCE_BODY_REJECTED` (a body/content field on a reference payload), 422
`TARGET_NOT_PUBLISHED` (an automation must name a published, unblocked Agent or Flow), 422 `PATH_OUTSIDE_ROOT`, 422 `PERSONAL_ITEM_NOT_PUBLISHED` (personal items never enter the work dataset), 413 `BULK_TOO_LARGE`
(> 200 items). WIP limits are soft in P0: a move past the limit succeeds and the response carries `wip_exceeded=true`; the choice is a `wip_limit_decision` with outcome `moved_anyway`.9:T7ac,HTTP contract of `apps/graph-api` (dev port 3205, image port 8000;
owner NexGraph). Payload shapes are the JSON Schema components `../schemas/v1/graph-*.schema.json` — not
duplicated here. This file is the ONLY API reference of NexGraph; the portal renders it later.

Every operation runs the PEP pipeline (authenticate → tenancy → OpenFGA → OPA → execute → audit/outbox) and declares
its action in `x-nexus-pep` (`GraphPepAction` vocabulary) `ActorContext` is derived server-side from the bearer or
the service token + propagated human context (grounding calls from chat-api); clients never send authority.
Administering ≠ reading: bindings, grants and runs are readable by administrators without a Project
relation as ids, kinds, states and counts only — titles, knowledge counts and any content require a Project relation.
Hidden and missing objects both return 404 (no existence leak). Denials return coarse `reason_code` +
`user_message_key` (`PepCheckResponse`), never policy text. `verified` / `falsified` are written by exactly one path:
an `OracleRun` (service oracle for deterministic checks, human curator for `human_review`) — no other operation
accepts these statuses. Text leaves graph-api only through `getItemText` / `getPageRender` /
`downloadSourceVersion`, each PEP-checked with classification clearance and `Content-Disposition: attachment`; `POST /graph/resolve` returns refs only. Fail closed: `AUTHZ_MODE=engine` without a valid
signed bundle ⇒ 503 `POLICY_UNAVAILABLE` on every protected operation.

Reason codes: 403 `AUTHOR_NOT_HUMAN` (verify by agent/service) · 403 `PEP_DENIED` · 404 `NOT_FOUND` (hidden or
missing) · 409 `CONNECTION_NEEDS_AUTH` · 409 `GRANT_EXPIRED` · 409 `RUN_NOT_RETRYABLE` · 413 `ITEM_TOO_LARGE` ·
415 `MIME_NOT_ALLOWED` · 422 `POLICY_WIDENS` · 422 `VALIDATION` · 503 `POLICY_UNAVAILABLE` · 503
`CAPABILITY_UNAVAILABLE` (a model role without a bound model — never a cloud fallback).a:Tde2,HTTP contract of `apps/bi-api` (dev port 3206, image port 8000;
owner NexBI). Payload shapes are the JSON Schema components `../schemas/v1/bi-*.schema.json` — not
duplicated here. This file is the ONLY API reference of NexBI; the portal renders it later.

Every operation runs the PEP pipeline (authenticate → tenancy → OpenFGA → OPA → execute → audit/outbox) and declares
its action in `x-nexus-pep` (`BiPepAction` vocabulary) — the PEP matrix in `` /
`evidence/uat-2/PEP-MATRIX.md` and this file are checked against each other by the contract test
(`epic010-s0-nexbi.test.ts`). `ActorContext` is derived server-side from the bearer or the service token + propagated
human context (chat-api's data arm, the Flow runtime's `dataset.publish`, the platform tool door); clients never send
authority. Operations marked `x-nexus-human-only: true` refuse agent and service identities with 403
`AUTHOR_NOT_HUMAN`: certify, publish (dashboard · dataset version), correct a cell, acknowledge a revocation.

The query door is one: `POST …/queries` takes a `BIQuery` (`bi-query.schema.json`) — metric refs,
dimensions, typed filters, time, comparison — and NEVER text or SQL; the deterministic compiler is the only SQL author;
the SQL leaves bi-api only through `explainQuery` (`query.view_sql`, Project editors). Every block of a dashboard is
computed per viewer at read time (`renderDashboard`); results are cached only per (policy-decision hash, query,
dataset version) — never shared across viewers (R-2). Administering ≠ reading: an
administrator without a Project relation reads `getOverview` with `content_visible=false` (counts only) and gets 404
on every content route. Hidden and missing objects both return 404. Denials return coarse `reason_code` +
`user_message_key`, never policy text. Fail closed: `AUTHZ_MODE=engine` without a valid signed bundle ⇒ 503
`POLICY_UNAVAILABLE` on every protected operation.

Reason codes: 403 `AUTHOR_NOT_HUMAN` · 403 `PEP_DENIED` · 404 `NOT_FOUND` · 409 `METRIC_KEY_TAKEN` · 409
`VERSION_REVOKED` · 409 `DASHBOARD_NOT_DRAFT` · 409 `ARTIFACT_FOREIGN_PROJECT` · 413 `UPLOAD_TOO_LARGE` (> 64 MiB)
· 415 `MIME_NOT_ALLOWED` · 422 `VALIDATION` · 422 `EXPRESSION_INVALID` (with position) · 422 `QUERY_UNRESOLVED`
(a metric/dimension not in the semantic model — never a guess) · 422 `SPEC_COPY_REJECTED` · 429
`QUERY_BUDGET` (per-tenant concurrency) · 503 `POLICY_UNAVAILABLE` · 503 `CAPABILITY_UNAVAILABLE` (a model role
without a bound model — never a cloud fallback) · 504 `QUERY_TIMEOUT` (statement_timeout).

**Two scopes, one contract (A1; `BiScopeKind`).** Every `/bi/projects/{project_id}/…`
operation below is also served under `/bi/organization/…` — the **organization scope**, the home of content that
measures the organization rather than a Project (the NEXUS platform dashboards, Vibeban work). Same payloads, same
`x-nexus-pep` actions; the scope only changes *who holds which relation*: a human with membership `admin` acts as
the scope's **owner** (edits, publishes, certifies, creates automations), a `member` as its **viewer** (reads
within the classification ceiling), a `guest`, an agent or a service on its own holds nothing (404 on content).
Project content stays behind Project relations ( untouched). Objects in the organization scope carry
`project_id: "organization"` on the wire — a reserved scope id, never a Project id; `/bi/projects/organization/…`
is refused with 404. Only the `platform` operations exist in the organization scope alone.12:X

24:T650,1.95.0 ( S1): role=assistant, `route.kind=platform_action` — the registered UI action the shell runs. `resolved`: the server decided the PEP action on the exact target and `href` is the App Launcher route to open (the client answers with route outcome `client_action.acked`); `choice`: two or more permitted targets — the person picks on screen (`client_action.picked`), never a guess; `not_found`: nothing the person may open by that name (the same words for a missing and a closed resource). `user_message_key` is `route.platform_action.<status>`. 1.108.0 (S4a): `proposed` — a level-2/3 action waits for the person (`proposal`; the card's key moves to confirmed · cancelled · superseded · expired); `done` · `denied` · `failed` — the message that reports the owning API's answer (`result`); `cancelled` · `expired` — the spoken/typed *no* or the lapse, said back; `needs_visual_confirmation` — level 4: the on-screen approval is the only door. 1.110.0 (S4b): `context_stale` — a confirm whose current snapshot differs materially from the proposal's is not applied; `choices` hold the earlier and the current resource and `POST …/decide {apply_to}` picks; `context_missing` — no snapshot behind *this / my screen*: the assistant says it sees no context and offers select · share · session-only file, never claiming to see anything; `context_access_changed` — the per-turn READ re-check of the snapshot's resource failed, nothing was answered from it; `answered` — a read action the platform answered from the owning runtime (`flow.explain_last_run`): the message `text` is the answer.25:T4bf,1.20.0 — the inbound webhook that starts ONE published flow (its `trigger.kind = webhook`, `trigger.webhook.hook_id`). PUBLIC (no bearer) — the signature IS the authentication. Headers `X-Nexus-Timestamp` (unix seconds) and `X-Nexus-Signature` (`sha256=<hex HMAC-SHA256 over "<timestamp>.<raw body>">` with the hook secret the broker resolves from `secret_ref`; constant-time compare; the previous secret stays valid for tolerance_s after a rotation). Body ≤ 256 KiB, `application/json` only. Accepted → the flow starts as its publisher's snapshot (`run_as_agent_uri` / owner), `FlowRun.trigger = {kind: webhook, source_ref: nexus://chat/webhook_receipt/<id>}`, `inputs.event = {headers (allow-listed: content-type, user-agent, x-nexus-*), body}`; 202 `{run_id}`; `nexus.chat.webhook.received.v1`. Refusals carry NO detail beyond the code — 401 WEBHOOK_SIGNATURE_INVALID · 401 WEBHOOK_STALE (|now − timestamp| > tolerance_s) · 409 WEBHOOK_REPLAY (same hook + signature inside replay_window_s) · 404 HOOK_NOT_FOUND (unknown, deprecated or disarmed hook) · 413 WEBHOOK_TOO_LARGE · 429 WEBHOOK_RATE_LIMITED (60 / min per hook).
